8. Limitations

Limitations (standing; updated as stages complete)

  • No real-data results yet. Every number produced so far comes from SYNTHETIC fixtures used to verify the implementation; they are labelled as such and are not scientific results.
  • Simplified reproduction. Compact model (1.8 M params vs MotionLM’s 9 M), far fewer training steps, a subset of WOMD shards, no NMS/k-means mode aggregation, six raw samples for local metrics. Not an official challenge submission; the official metrics package could not be installed here (DECISIONS D-019a), so the custom metrics are audited on analytic cases and oracles only.
  • Benchmark-provided targets. The two targets are the dataset’s objects_of_interest pair; this is evaluation metadata, not an onboard target-selection algorithm.
  • Open-loop context. Context agents are not simulated; only the two targets interact (2 Hz joint autoregression). Interpolating 2 Hz outputs to 10 Hz for geometric checks does not make the model a reactive 10 Hz policy.
  • Synthetic corruption. Missing frames and Gaussian position noise are stress scenarios, not calibrated Waymo sensor-error distributions; they say nothing about raw camera/LiDAR perception.
  • Offroad metric. Road-edge polylines with a sign convention calibrated by the log-playback oracle; vehicles only; centre-point test (no corner test); invalid maps excluded and counted.
  • Inference unit. Scenario-level paired bootstrap; WOMD does not expose the originating run segment, so independence between windows is assumed, not verified.
  • Seeds. Single-seed evidence unless the pilot budget supports three seeds (planned: 3).
  • RL scope. REINFORCE with LOO baseline/KL/replay over two agents and a 16-step episode; rewards are deterministic generated-trajectory diagnostics. Differs from the all-agent closed-loop setting of the Waymo RL paper.
  • Source-segment overlap. Identified only through SDC motion n-gram matching (D-025); train scenes sharing a segment with dev were removed, final shares none, but same-segment windows remain inside each split, so the scenario-level bootstrap slightly overstates independence.
  • Overlap-metric reference rate. On jointly valid intervals the 2 Hz-waypoint OBB test flags 1.0 % of logged joint futures on dev (D-028a: 3 annotation-level vehicle–pedestrian contacts, 2 heading-inference artefacts). The full-horizon rate on logged futures is undefined (invalid labels carry no geometry); model overlap_rate (full horizon) and overlap_rate_jointvalid (identical intervals as the logged reference) are both reported with their denominators.
  • Mixture training as regulariser. The pilot over-fits after ≈ 30 epochs (D-027); corruption mixture training also augments the data, so part of any M1 gain may be regularisation rather than corruption awareness. The matched extra-SFT control and clean guardrail bound, but do not remove, this confound.
  • Overlap reference rate is not a lower bound (D-028a). It is the same approximate test applied to logs; it mixes annotation contacts and heading-inference artefacts, and vehicle–pedestrian pairs dominate it. The RL overlap reward is therefore an approximate geometric-consistency objective, not a verified safety measure.
  • Bootstrap unit. CIs resample heuristic source-segment groups (D-025a); the grouping is derived from SDC motion matching and cannot guarantee independence.
  • RL outcome (D-031). Bounded REINFORCE post-training lowered box overlap but worsened clean accuracy by 18–27 % and diversity, with ≈ 8× more excessive stopping; by the frozen rule the RL effect does not count, and the inference-time candidate-selection control dominates it. Reward curves during training are not held-out improvements.
  • Timings. Seconds-per-scene figures were measured on a GPU shared with unrelated jobs; they are descriptive and do not rank methods by speed. Sampling counts per scene are the cost driver.
